We need strong normalization for CBV STLC with locally nameless syntax.

Standard approach: Define a unary logical relation / reducibility predicate:
- `R T t` := for T = Bool: `SN t` (or `SN t /\ (t = true \/ t = false \/ ...)`). Actually for strong normalization we want "t is SN" and additionally for arrow types: t is SN, and whenever t -->* v with v a value (abs), then for all u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app v u). But careful with CBV.

Actually a standard "hereditarily strongly normalizing" definition:

For CBV STLC, simplest approach: Define `sn t := strongly_normalizing t`. Then define the logical relation:

CR Bool t := SN t  — actually we need something that's preserved by reduction and that for closed（指没有自由变量） terms implies SN. But for the fundamental theorem, we need: for application case, if t1 : T1 -> T2 and t2 : T1, then t1 t2 is SN. （这么早就明白了application case的设计要求）

Hmm, with just SN for Bool, the application case: t1 : T1 -> T2 is SN and t2 is SN and both are... We need to know that when t1 reduces to a value (abs), and t2 to a value, the app reduces to `open t v` which should be SN. That requires closure under beta: if t and v are SN, then open t v is SN. Hmm, but that's not true in general for open terms with free variables — actually with locally nameless and free variables, SN of terms with free variables is fine: free variables are SN (no reduction), and open t v = substituting v for bvar 0. If t is SN and v is SN, is open t v SN? That's a substitution lemma: SN is closed under substitution. That's true for SN in the sense "every term all of whose substitution instances are SN"? Hmm, careful: SN as defined here is a property of the specific term; a term with a free variable is SN because there's no reduction. But substituting a non-SN term for a free variable could yield non-SN. E.g. t = fvar x is SN but open... well, in locally nameless, substitution is `open t v` for bvar 0.

Actually the standard issue: SN is not closed under substitution naively, e.g., t = tm_bvar 0 is SN (no reduction steps), but open t (omega) = omega which is not SN. So we need the "strongly normalizing" predicate on all substitution instances. Standard approach uses the reducibility candidate that includes "all substitution instances are SN", or one uses the "hereditarily SN" with a Kripke/term-variable interpretation.

The task says "construct an appropriate unary, type-indexed logical relation; define the term-variable context/substitution interpretation". So we need context interpretation.

Alternative simpler approach for STLC strong normalization: Use Girard's reducibility candidates with "neutral" terms, or use the "SN is closed under substitution when the substituted terms are SN"? Let's check that lemma:（模型尝试这个定理：如果原程序是 SN，用来替换自由变量的程序也是 SN，那么替换后的程序仍然 SN。它想用这个引理补上 APP 分支中“各自 SN → 调用后也 SN”的缺口。但即使暂且假设这个引理成立，也不能单靠它填上：它需要“body 是 SN ＋ 参数是 SN”，而 APP 分支目前给的是“被调用的整个程序是 SN ＋ 参数是 SN”。整个 lambda 是 SN，不代表它的 body 是 SN，因为这里的 CBV 不在 lambda 内归约。因此还缺“body 为什么 SN”这一环。另外，这个候选引理本身不成立。）

Standard approach: define the logical relation over *closed* terms? But the fundamental theorem needs to handle open terms: `open t1 (fvar x)` under context `x |-> T1; Gamma`. So the relation must be defined for terms with free variables, and the context interpretation maps each free var to a relation on terms; the semantic judgment `Gamma |= t \in T` means: for all substitutions sigma (maps each var x to a term v_x?) ... Actually simpler: use the standard "all substitution instances" approach where we treat free variables via a context interpretation with a *valuation*: semantic typing `Gamma |= t \in T` := for all sigma such that for each x, Gamma x = Some S implies R S (sigma x), we have R T (subst sigma t), where subst replaces free variables with terms. Alternatively, the "hereditarily SN" approach: Define `R T t` as a predicate that includes all substitution instances: since typing gives us free variables only via T_Var, and the fundamental theorem is done by induction on typing derivation with a context interpretation, we need a substitution lemma for the logical relation (if R T1 v and Gamma, x:T1 |= t : T2 then Gamma |= t[x:=v] : T2). With locally nameless, `t[x:=v]` = subst_fvar.

Actually, here's a cleaner formulation, avoiding fvar substitution entirely by using a *context of fvars* interpretation where each fvar is mapped to a term value, and substitution lemma proved for fvars. Since locally nameless terms have fvars as atoms, we can define:

```
Fixpoint subst_fvar (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst_fvar sigma t1) (subst_fvar sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst_fvar sigma t1)   (* no capture issue: nameless binders *)
  ...
```

So we need: R (T1->T2) t -> R T1 u -> R T2 (app t u), for closed t, u (locally closed). This is the crux. Let's prove it with a well-founded induction on SN t and SN u.

Let me think about the proof of the application lemma for closed terms, using SN of t and u.

Goal: prove R T2 (app t u) where R (T1->T2) t and R T1 u.
By definition, if T2 = Bool: SN (app t u). If T2 = Arrow T2a T2b: SN (app t u) ∧ ∀ t', app t u -->* t', value t', ∀ w ∈ R T2a, R T2b (app t' w).

Hmm, the second part says: app t u reduces to a value v (an abstraction), and we need its applications to w in R T2a to be in R T2b. Note that R (T1->T2) t's clause gives: for t' value with t -->* t' and u ∈ R T1: R T2 (app t' u). But app t u reducing to a value v: what values can app t u reach? Since the language is CBV STLC (no if), applications of non-abstractions get stuck. If it reduces to a value v, then... v is an abs. Which abs? It should be of the form: t reduces to an abs (a value), and u reduces to a value w, and then open body w. Hmm, but multi-step could interleave: t reduces multiple steps to abs, then u reduces to a value, then beta. Actually with CBV, app t u -->* v implies t -->* abs T0 body, u -->* uval, and v is reached from open body uval by further reduction. Hmm, is that exactly right? The reduction of app t u: either App1 (reduce t) or App2 (t already a value, reduce u) or AppAbs. So to reach a value, t must first reduce to a value (abs), then u to a value, then AppAbs produces open body uval, then further reductions of that. Could open body uval reduce further to another value. So v is a value reachable from open body uval.

Then to show R T2b (app v w), use: R (T2a->T2b) v? Hmm, we know R T2 (app t' u) for t'=abs and u ∈ R T1 — i.e., R T2 (app (abs T0 body) u) where u is our R T1 term (which reduces to uval). Hmm wait, here the "u" in R T2 (app t' u) is the same arbitrary R T1 term. So R T2 (app abs u) gives the clause: for all u', app abs u -->* u', value u', R T2b applied... So at u' = v (a value reachable from app abs u), we get ∀ w ∈ R T2a, R T2b (app v w). Exactly what we need!

So the needed lemma is circular-ish but can be structured via a well-founded induction on SN t and SN u.

FT: forall Gamma t T (H : has_type Gamma t T), forall sigma, (forall x S, Gamma x = Some S -> R S (sigma x)) -> R T (subst sigma t).

So define:
```
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.
```

Since T2 is a subterm of T, structural induction on T works. ✓

But careful: R_step needs LC facts: given t --> t', LC t' from LC t (LC preserved by step; we'll need a lemma). And for the arrow clause: R T2 (app t u) → app t u --> app t' u (ST_App1 with LC u) → R T2 (app t' u) via IH_T2. ✓

So R_step : forall T t t', R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t'. Induction on T.
- Bool: destruct R as (LC, SN). LC t'? Need step preserves LC. SN t'? From SN t: SN_intro inversion. ✓
- Arrow: R = (LC t, SN t, H). Need (LC t', SN t', ∀ u, R T1 u → R T2 (app t' u)). LC t' via step LC preservation. SN t' via SN inversion. For the forall: given u with R T1 u, need R T2 (app t' u). 3. Lemma: step preserves LC: lc_at 0 t -> t --> t' -> lc_at 0 t'.

   - ST_App1: t = app t1 t2, t' = app t1' t2; LC t1' from IH, LC t2 given. ✓
   - ST_App2: similarly.

6. Lemma: substitution (fvar) preserves LC: lc_at 0 t -> (forall x, lc_at 0 (sigma x)) -> lc_at 0 (subst sigma t). Since fvars are always LC, actually subst doesn't affect bvars: any fvar becomes sigma x which must be LC. So yes.

   Wait: does subst ever introduce bvars that could be captured? With locally nameless, no capture. subst replaces fvar x with sigma x; if sigma x has free bvars, those become free bvars in the result, breaking LC. But if sigma x is LC, then no free bvars. ✓

7. Lemma: subst/open commutation: subst sigma (open body (fvar x)) with x fresh and sigma' = update sigma x w ... Let me define substitution and prove:
   `subst_open_fvar : forall sigma x w body, ~ In x (fvars body) -> subst (update sigma x w) (open body (tm_fvar x)) = open (subst sigma body) w`.
   Hmm, actually we need x fresh in body AND also that other fvars in body not x get sigma. Note: `update sigma x w` at y: if eqb x y then w else sigma y. And open (subst sigma body) w: replaces bvar 0 with w. The LHS: open body (fvar x) replaces free bvar 0 with fvar x, then subst maps fvar x to w (and other fvars via sigma). If body had an fvar x already, LHS would replace it with w but RHS keeps sigma x. Hence freshness needed. But note also: open (subst sigma body) w: subst sigma body replaces fvars, leaves bvars; then open replaces free bvar 0. Both operations commute appropriately.

   Actually, do we even need freshness? For the T_Abs case, we pick x fresh w.r.t. t1 and L. If t1 has no fvar x, then LHS/RHS agree. But careful: we need ~ In x (fvars t1) where fvars t1 = all fvars in t1 including under binders. Since x ranges over atoms (nat) and t1 has finitely many fvars, we can pick fresh.

   Also, we need a freshness lemma to produce x not in L ∪ fvars(t1). Standard: exists x, ~ In x (L ++ fvars t1). We can use a simple construction: pick a bound larger than all atoms in the list. Or use a lemma like `not_in_list` from lists. We can write our own: for any list of atoms l, exists x, ~ In x l. Classic: use max of list + 1 or construct by induction. E.g., `exists x, ~ In x l` proven by induction on l: []: x=0; (a::l): by IH get x not in l; if x ≠ a then done, else pick x+1? But x+1 might equal some element of l. Hmm, standard approach: define `fresh l := fold_right (fun a acc => max (S a) acc) 0 l`? Simpler: use `max_list`? Stdlib's List has `max_list`? There's `list_max`? Not sure. Alternative: prove exists x, ~ In x l by induction on l with a stronger statement: exists x, (forall y, In y l -> y < x). This is easy: [] → x=1; a::l → IH gives x with all y in l < x; take max (S a) x. Then ~ In x l since x ≥ S a > a and x > all y. Let me implement.

   Actually simpler: the standard "atom_fresh" lemma: `exists x, ~ In x L` can be proven using `max` over the list via `fold_right`. Let's write:
   ```
   Fixpoint max_atom (l : list atom) : atom :=
     match l with [] => 0 | a :: l' => Nat.max a (max_atom l') end.
   Lemma max_atom_spec : forall l a, In a l -> a <= max_atom l.
   Lemma fresh_atom : forall l, ~ In (S (max_atom l)) l.
   ```
   Good.

Actually Coq's scheme for non-uniform/nested: The induction principle for `has_type` will be:
```
has_type_ind : forall P : context -> tm -> ty -> Prop,
  (forall Gamma x T, Gamma x = Some T -> P Gamma (fvar x) T) ->
  (forall L Gamma T1 T2 t1,
     (forall x, ~ In x L -> P (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2) ->
     P Gamma (abs T1 t1) (T1 -> T2)) ->
  ...
```
Yes, Coq automatically lifts IHs through forall/arrow premises. Good.

Let me now also handle the LC lemmas carefully. Let's define:

```
Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom := ...
```

And substitution:
```
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.
```

LC lemmas:
- `lc_at_mono : lc_at k t -> k <= k' -> lc_at k' t`. Hmm, is that true? lc_at k t means bvar i bound requires i < k + depth. If k grows, still holds: i < k <= k'. Yes. Prove by induction on t (or on the LC derivation).
  Actually we might need: lc_at 0 t -> lc_at k t for any k. Monotonic ✓.

- `lc_open : forall k body v, lc_at (S k) body -> lc_at k v -> lc_at k (open_rec k v body)`.
  Proof by induction on body with general k. Cases:
  - bvar i: if i =? k then v else bvar i. If i = k: need lc_at k v ✓. Else: need lc_at k (bvar i), requires i < k. From lc_at (S k) (bvar i) we get i < S k, i.e., i ≤ k; with i ≠ k, i < k ✓.
  - fvar: lc_fvar ✓.
  - app: IH.
  - abs T t1: need lc_at k (abs T (open_rec (S k) v t1)) = lc_abs (lc_at (S (S k))? hmm). Wait: open_rec k v (abs T t1) = abs T (open_rec (S k) v t1). Need lc_at k (abs T (open_rec (S k) v t1)) which by lc_abs needs lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) v t1). Hypothesis: lc_at (S k) (abs T t1) → lc_at (S (S k)) t1.   - true/false ✓.
  Generalize over k and v in induction.

- `lc_at_weaken` (mono) as above. Actually for the abs case we need lc_at k v -> lc_at (S k) v, i.e. mono.

- `lc_open_fvar_inv : forall k body x, lc_at k (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) body) -> lc_at (S k) body`. 
  Proof by induction on body generalizing k. Cases:
  - bvar i: open_rec k (fvar x) (bvar i) = if i =? k then fvar x else bvar i. If i = k: lc_at k (fvar x) gives nothing about i < S k? We need lc_at (S k) (bvar k) = i < S k = k < S k ✓. If i ≠ k: lc_at k (bvar i) gives i < k < S k ✓.

  - app: IH both.
  - abs: lc_at k (abs T (open_rec (S k) (fvar x) t1)) → lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) (fvar x) t1) → IH with k := S k gives lc_at (S (S k)) t1 → lc_abs gives lc_at (S k) (abs T t1) ✓.
  - true/false ✓.

- `lc_subst : forall sigma t, lc_at 0 t -> (forall x, lc_at 0 (sigma x)) -> lc_at 0 (subst sigma t)`. More generally lc_at k preserved: lc_at k t → (∀x, lc_at k (sigma x)) → lc_at k (subst sigma t).   - true/false ✓.

  - ST_AppAbs: t = app (abs T body) v. H : lc_at 0 (app (abs T body) v) → lc_at 0 (abs T body) and lc_at 0 v. lc_at 0 (abs T body) → lc_at 1 body. `lc_open 0 body v : lc_at 1 body -> lc_at 0 v -> lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 v body)` = lc_at 0 (open body v) ✓.

Now, let's think about whether R_sn needs LC etc. Define:
```
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.
```
Coq's termination: R recurses on T's structure; T1 and T2 are subterms ✓. But note the recursive call `R T2 (tm_app t u)` inside a forall — that's fine for Fixpoint? The recursion is on T (T2 is a direct subterm), and the recursive occurrence appears in the body; Coq accepts nested recursion in any position as long as structurally decreasing. Actually, `Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) {struct T} : Prop := match T with ... R T1 u ... R T2 (app t u) ... end` — yes, structurally recursive on T. Accepted.

Hmm, but there might be an issue with the positivity/guard checking? No, it's a Fixpoint returning Prop; fine.

Now R_sn:
```
Lemma R_sn : forall T t, R T t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  induction T; intros t HR; simpl in HR.
  - destruct HR as [Hlc Hsn]; exact Hsn.
  - destruct HR as [Hlc [Hsn Hcl]]; exact Hsn.
Qed.
```
Careful: `induction T` with T as a ty; the goal `forall t, R T t -> SN t`. Since R is a Fixpoint, `simpl` unfolds given T's constructor. Actually need to destruct the R hypothesis after simplifying. If T = Ty_Bool, R = lc ∧ SN; if arrow, lc ∧ SN ∧ clause. ✓ But is `induction T` valid? We don't even need induction; just `intros T t HR; destruct T as [|T1 T2]; simpl in HR; ...`. But does `simpl` unfold R? R is a Fixpoint applied to a constructor, so yes it reduces. Let's just do `destruct T; simpl in *`.

R_step:
```
Lemma R_step : forall T t t', R T t -> step t t' -> R T t'.
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t t' HR Hstep; simpl in *.
  - destruct HR as [Hlc Hsn]. split.
    + eapply step_lc; eauto.
    + inversion Hsn as [?? Hsucc]; apply Hsucc; assumption.
  - destruct HR as [Hlc [Hsn Hcl]]. repeat split.
    + eapply step_lc; eauto.
    + inversion Hsn as [?? Hsucc]; apply Hsucc; assumption.

Let's think about the actual semantic proof of strong normalization for STLC. The standard "hereditarily terminating" relation is defined on *closed* terms, and the key lemma is that if t is hereditarily terminating at arrow type then t u is hereditarily terminating at T2 for any hereditarily terminating u. This is *by definition* of the arrow case in many formulations. Then the lambda case: abs is HT at T1->T2 if for every HT u at T1, abs u is HT at T2. And abs u is HT at T2 is proven by showing it's strongly normalizing... For nested types, this requires the inductive structure of "HT at T2" which is defined recursively: HT at arrow = SN ∧ ∀ HT u, HT at T2 (app t u). So proving HT (abs body) at arrow type T2 = T2a -> T2b requires: SN(abs u) ∧ ∀ w ∈ HT T2a, HT T2b (app (abs u) w). And this is not just SN. So the standard proof needs to show HT T2b (app (abs u) w) for arbitrary HT w. Hmm.

How do textbook proofs handle this? Girard's reducibility candidates: The candidate for arrow type is defined via quantification over all candidates of the argument type, and the key property is that a term belongs if all its applications... Actually, in Girard's definition, reducible terms of arrow type are those t such that for all reducible u, t u is reducible. And the lambda case is proven by showing: for all u reducible, (λx.body) u is reducible. Since `(λx.body) u` reduces in one step to body[x:=u], and the set of reducible terms is closed under "reduction backward" (if t' is reducible and t --> t' with t "neutral-ish"? there's the CR3 condition), which requires t to be neutral. Hmm, Girard's CR3: if t is neutral and all its one-step reducts are reducible then t is reducible. In our setting the definition is different.

Actually with strong normalization defined as "all reduction sequences finite" (rather than a reducibility candidate with conditions), the direct approach is: show that all reducts of the term are SN by induction. For app (app abs u) w, the term is an application whose left part is an application. Reductions can occur in three places. Standard "SN is closed under application" lemmas handle this via induction on the SN structure.

Maybe a cleaner formulation: prove SN of app (app abs u) w directly via well-founded induction on SN u and SN w, using strong normalization of open body u and open body... Hmm.

Let me look at this from the "reducibility candidates" perspective. In Girard's framework with SN defined as an inductive "all reducts strongly normalizing", the standard CR for arrow type is: CR_{T1->T2} = { t | ∀ u ∈ CR_{T1}, t u ∈ CR_{T2} } possibly with SN condition. And the key lemma is: if body is reducible under x:T1 (i.e., ∀ u ∈ CR_{T1}, body[x:=u] ∈ CR_{T2}) then λx.body ∈ CR_{T1->T2}. Proof: let u ∈ CR_{T1}; need (λx.body) u ∈ CR_{T2}. Use CR3 (neutral + all one-step reducts reducible ⇒ reducible). The one-step reducts are body[x:=u] (reducible by hypothesis — wait, only when u is a value? In full beta, (λx.body) u --> body[x:=u] always; in CBV, only when u is a value). And other reducts are (λx.body') u where body --> body' — but CBV doesn't reduce under binders, so no. So one-step reducts = {body[x:=u]} when u value. And we need CR3: neutral term all of whose reducts are reducible is reducible.

So the key is CR3 and the closure condition. In our setting where R is defined recursively with SN and application clause, we can prove a lemma:

"Neutral expansion": If t is an application-like term (not a value) such that all its one-step reducts are in R T, and t is SN, then t ∈ R T? Hmm, that's essentially the definition for arrow types plus SN.

Let's try: For T = Bool, R Bool = SN, so t ∈ R Bool iff SN t: if all one-step reducts are SN, then SN t ✓ (SN_intro).

What are the natural conditions on t and u? We want: t is semantically of type T1->T2, u of type T1, and also t is "abs-related to body" to handle the beta step. In the lambda case, t = abs T1 body and we have Hbody : ∀w ∈ R T1, R T2 (open body w). So the lemma should be:

In the arrow case T2 = T2a->T2b, the third component requires R T2b (app (app abs u) w) — for this, maybe we can invoke the *induction hypothesis at T2b* with a *different body*: we need a body' and u' with app (abs T1' body') u' = app (app abs u) w. That's not syntactically of that form... unless u' = w and abs T1' body' = app abs u — but the left part of the application must be an abstraction, and it's an application. So no.

Hmm, so induction on T2 doesn't directly work. What if we prove R T2b (app (app abs u) w) via the arrow clause of R T2b's *definition*? For T2b an arrow... no.

New idea: Maybe the strict definition R arrow := SN ∧ ∀u, R T1 u → R T2 (app t u) is problematic precisely because R's arrow clause applies to non-values. What if we restrict the clause to values: R (T1->T2) t := LC t ∧ SN t ∧ ∀ v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (app t v)? Then in T_App case, the argument t2 may not be a value. But wait: in CBV, if t2 is not a value, then app t1 t2 reduces (App2 or App1). So maybe the T_App case of the fundamental theorem can be handled by a different lemma: given R arrow t1 and R T1 t2, prove R T2 (app t1 t2) by induction on SN t1 and SN t2, using the "value-restricted" arrow clause. Let's explore this.

With R arrow restricted to values, the nested issue might disappear: In the lambda case, to prove R (T1->T2) (abs body), we need ∀ v value ∈ R T1, R T2 (app abs v). Then app abs v is an application of an abstraction to a value; its reducts are exactly open body v (and also app abs v is stuck otherwise). So R T2 (app abs v) can be proven by *backward closure* from R T2 (open body v): all one-step reducts of app abs v are in R T2 (just open body v), and SN follows. But for T2 = arrow, R T2 (app abs v) needs the ∀q value clause: R T2b (app (app abs v) q) for q a value in R T2a. Hmm, again nested. Unless we prove a general lemma: "if all one-step reducts of t are in R T then t ∈ R T" (backward closure) — for T arrow this requires knowing R T2b (app t' w) for reducts t' — but app (app abs v) q's reducts: app (open body v) q (since app abs v --> open body v, via App1) — and q is a value, so app (open body v) q: is it in R T2b? We have R (T2a->T2b) (open body v) from Hbody v, so its clause (if restricted to values) gives R T2b (app (open body v) q) ✓ (q is a value in R T2a). So the backward closure lemma for app (app abs v) q: all one-step reducts are in R T2b (the only reduct is app (open body v) q via App1 — wait, also App2 requires the function side to be a value; app abs v is not a value; AppAbs requires function side to be an abs; app abs v is an app). So the only reduct is app (open body v) q, which is in R T2b ✓. Then by backward closure, R T2b (app (app abs v) q) ✓.

So with the value-restricted arrow clause, backward closure might work beautifully! The key lemma: "backward closure" (CR3-like): if all one-step reducts of t are in R T and t is SN, then t ∈ R T.

Wait, but that's not right in general: consider T = Bool, R Bool = SN; t with all reducts SN ⟹ SN t ✓. For T = arrow T1->T2 (value-restricted): assume SN t and ∀ v value ∈ R T1, R T2 (app t v). We need to prove R T2 (app t v) for value v ∈ R T1 — that's given directly! Oh nice: with the value-restricted definition, R (T1->T2) t := LC t ∧ SN t ∧ ∀ v, value v → R T1 v → R T2 (app t v). Then to prove R T (app abs v) for T arrow: need LC, SN, and ∀ q value ∈ R T1', R T2b (app (app abs v) q). We just showed R T2b (app (app abs v) q) via backward closure needs: all one-step reducts of app (app abs v) q are in R T2b, and SN.

      ```

      ```
      Lemma R_backward : forall T t,
        lc_at 0 t -> SN t -> (forall t', step t t' -> R T t') -> (forall T0 body, t <> tm_abs T0 body) -> R T t.

  Now the induction on typing for the fundamental theorem. Let's write out the full proof plan in Coq.

  Definitions and lemmas:

  ```
  (* free variables of a term *)
  Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
    match t with
    | tm_bvar _ => nil
    | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
    | tm_app t1 t2 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2
    | tm_abs _ t1 => fvars t1
    | tm_true => nil
    | tm_false => nil
    end.

  (* max of a list of atoms *)
  Fixpoint max_atom (l : list atom) : atom :=
    match l with
    | nil => 0
    | a :: l' => Nat.max a (max_atom l')
    end.

  Lemma max_atom_spec : forall l a, In a l -> a <= max_atom l.
  Proof. induction l; intros; destruct H; subst; simpl; [apply Nat.le_max_l | apply Nat.le_trans with (max_atom l); auto; apply Nat.le_max_r]. Qed.
  ```
  Hmm, need `Nat.max` lemmas: Nat.le_max_l : forall n m, n <= Nat.max n m; Nat.le_max_r. ✓ (PeanoNat imported.)

  ```
  Lemma fresh_atom : forall l, ~ In (S (max_atom l)) l.
  Proof. intros l H. pose proof (max_atom_spec l (S (max_atom l)) H). apply Nat.nlt_some? ... lia. Qed.
  ```

  Substitution:
  ```
  Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm := ...
  
  Definition subst_id : atom -> tm := fun x => tm_fvar x.

  Lemma subst_open_fvar : forall (sigma : atom -> tm) x w t,
      ~ In x (fvars t) ->
      subst (update sigma x w) (open t (tm_fvar x)) = open (subst sigma t) w.

  Wait, `update` is defined as `fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then Some T else Gamma y` — that's the *context* update (atom -> option ty), not a substitution update. I need a separate definition for substitutions:
  ```
  Definition subst_update (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (w : tm) : atom -> tm :=
    fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then w else sigma y.
  ```
  Careful about the eqb direction: update Gamma x T y = if Nat.eqb x y then Some T else Gamma y. So subst_update sigma x w y = if Nat.eqb x y then w else sigma y. Then subst_update sigma x w x = if eqb x x then w else ... = w ✓ (Nat.eqb_refl). And for y ≠ x, subst_update sigma x w y = sigma y (need eqb x y = false; Nat.eqb_neq). ✓

  Lemma subst_open_fvar proof: induction on t; generalize over the "k" depth? Hmm, `open t u = open_rec 0 u t`. The commutation: subst sigma' (open_rec 0 (fvar x) t) = open_rec 0 w (subst sigma t). Here subst doesn't care about depth (fvars are free). Let me prove by induction on t (with k fixed at 0? but the abs case uses open_rec (S k)...). Generalize: forall k, subst sigma' (open_rec k (fvar x) t) = open_rec k w (subst sigma t), given ~ In x (fvars t). Prove by induction on t, generalizing k:
  - bvar i: open_rec k (fvar x) (bvar i) = if eqb k i then fvar x else bvar i. 
    LHS = subst sigma' (if eqb k i then fvar x else bvar i) = if eqb k i then subst sigma' (fvar x) else bvar i = if eqb k i then w else bvar i.
    RHS = open_rec k w (subst sigma (bvar i)) = open_rec k w (bvar i) = if eqb k i then w else bvar i ✓.
    Wait: subst sigma' (fvar x) = sigma' x = if eqb x x then w else sigma x = w ✓ (using Nat.eqb_refl).
  - tm_fvar y: open_rec k (fvar x) (fvar y) = fvar y. LHS = subst sigma' (fvar y) = if eqb x y then w else sigma y. RHS = open_rec k w (subst sigma (fvar y)) = open_rec k w (sigma y) = sigma y (since open_rec on sigma y? Wait, open_rec k w (sigma y) — sigma y is an arbitrary term; open_rec would replace bvars equal to k in it! Hmm! That's wrong. This is the classic capture issue: open_rec k w (subst sigma t) where subst sigma (fvar y) = sigma y which may contain bvar k; open_rec would then replace those bvars with w — but LHS substitutes fvar y with sigma y and doesn't open it. So the equality fails unless sigma y has no bvar k... 

    Hmm. Right: opening after substitution vs substitution after opening don't commute in general. The standard lemma requires that sigma maps fvars to terms that are "closed enough" (no fvars? no, they can contain... hmm).

    Wait, actually let me reconsider: the standard lemma is `subst (open t u) = open (subst t) (subst u)` in named syntax, but for locally nameless, opening at bvar 0 with a term w and then substituting fvars: open_rec 0 w (subst sigma t) replaces bvars 0 in subst sigma t — these bvars come from t (not from sigma) if sigma's images are locally closed at level 0? If sigma y are locally closed (no free bvars at all), then open_rec won't affect them... but open_rec replaces bvars with index equal to the current depth k; if sigma y has no free bvars at all (LC), then at any depth d, its bvars are < d, so open_rec d w (sigma y) = sigma y ✓. So if all sigma y are locally closed, the commutation holds.

    Alternatively, formulate the substitution as capture-avoiding in a way that commutes: since sigma maps to locally closed terms (which is our use case: values in R are LC), the commutation holds.

    So lemma: if forall y, lc_at 0 (sigma y), ~ In x (fvars t), and lc_at 0 w, then subst (subst_update sigma x w) (open t (fvar x)) = open (subst sigma t) w.

    Hmm, but proving this requires care because during the induction, the "open_rec k" depth increases, and we need: open_rec k w (sigma y) = sigma y for d = k, which requires lc_at k (sigma y) ← from lc_at 0 (sigma y) by monotonicity ✓. Good.

    ```
    Lemma subst_open_rec_fvar : forall sigma x w t k,
      (forall y, lc_at 0 (sigma y)) ->
      lc_at 0 w ->
      ~ In x (fvars t) ->
      subst (subst_update sigma x w) (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t) = open_rec k w (subst sigma t).
    ```
    Proof by induction on t, generalizing k.
    - bvar i: as above (uses eqb).
    - fvar y: LHS = subst sigma' (fvar y) = if eqb x y then w else sigma y. RHS = open_rec k w (sigma y) = sigma y (by LC: need lemma open_rec_lc : lc_at k t -> open_rec k u t = t). And since ~ In x (fvars (fvar y)) means x ≠ y, eqb x y = false, so LHS = sigma y ✓.
    - app: IH.
    - abs T t1: LHS = subst sigma' (abs T (open_rec (S k) (fvar x) t1)) = abs T (subst sigma' (open_rec (S k) (fvar x) t1)). RHS = open_rec k w (abs T (subst sigma t1)) = abs T (open_rec (S k) w (subst sigma t1)). Apply IH with k := S k ✓. Freshness: ~ In x (fvars (abs T t1)) = ~ In x (fvars t1) ✓.
    - true/false ✓.

  - ST_AppAbs case: Hlc : lc_at 0 (tm_app (tm_abs T t) v). Need lc_at 0 (open t v). Inversion on Hlc gives lc_at 0 (tm_abs T t) and lc_at 0 v. Inversion on lc_at 0 (tm_abs T t) gives lc_at 1 t.   - ST_App1: Hlc : lc_at 0 (app t1 t2) → lc_at 0 t1, lc_at 0 t2. IHHst H1 : lc_at 0 t1'. lc_app ✓.
  - ST_App2: similar.

  Note: in ST_App1, the term being stepped is tm_app t1 t2 → tm_app t1' t2 ✓.

  Now, `R_step` needs `lc_at 0 u` where R T1 u. And the ST_App1 construction requires `locally_closed t2` where t2 = u ✓.

  Theorem normalization : forall t T, <{ empty |-- t \in T }> -> strongly_normalizing t.
  Proof.
    intros t T H.

  The context interpretation for empty: forall x S, empty x = Some S -> R S (subst_id x). empty x = None; so Some S = None impossible: `intros x S Hx; unfold empty in Hx; discriminate Hx.` ✓
  Then HR : R T (subst subst_id t). Rewrite subst_id_eq in HR. Apply R_sn ✓.

  ```
  has_type_ind :
    forall P : context -> tm -> ty -> Prop,
      (forall (Gamma : context) (x : atom) (T : ty), Gamma x = Some T -> P Gamma (tm_fvar x) T) ->
      (forall (L : list atom) (Gamma : context) (T1 T2 : ty) (t1 : tm),
          (forall x : atom, ~ In x L -> P (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (tm_fvar x)) T2) ->
          P Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (Ty_Arrow T1 T2)) ->
      (forall (Gamma : context) (t1 t2 : tm) (T1 T2 : ty),
          P Gamma t1 (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) -> P Gamma t2 T1 -> P Gamma (tm_app t1 t2) T2) ->
      (forall Gamma : context, P Gamma tm_true Ty_Bool) ->
      (forall Gamma : context, P Gamma tm_false Ty_Bool) ->
      forall (Gamma : context) (t : tm) (T : ty), has_type Gamma t T -> P Gamma t T.
  ```
  Yes, standard. So `induction H as [Gamma x T Hx | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 IH | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 | Gamma | Gamma]` should work.

  Lemma open_rec_lc : forall k t u, lc_at k t -> open_rec k u t = t.
  Proof by induction on t generalizing k and u:
  - bvar i: lc_at k (bvar i) → i < k → eqb k i = false (since i < k means i ≠ k; need (k =? i) = false: Nat.eqb_neq, k ≠ i; from i < k, k ≠ i ✓). Then open_rec k u (bvar i) = if k =? i then u else bvar i = bvar i ✓.
  - fvar ✓, true/false ✓.
  - app: open_rec k u (app t1 t2) = app (open_rec k u t1) (open_rec k u t2); lc_at k gives lc_at k t1, lc_at k t2; IH ✓.
  - abs: open_rec k u (abs T t1) = abs T (open_rec (S k) u t1); lc_at k gives lc_at (S k) t1; IH with k := S k ✓.

  Lemma lc_at_mono : forall k k' t, k <= k' -> lc_at k t -> lc_at k' t.
  Proof by induction on t generalizing k k'; the bvar case needs i < k <= k' ✓; abs case: lc_at (S k) t1 → lc_at (S k') t1 via IH with S k <= S k' ✓; others trivial.
  
  Actually can we prove it by induction on the lc_at derivation? Either way.

  Lemma lc_open_rec : forall k body v, lc_at (S k) body -> lc_at k v -> lc_at k (open_rec k v body).
  Induction on body generalizing k v:
  - bvar i: open_rec k v (bvar i) = if k =? i then v else bvar i.
    Case eqb true: i = k, so result v, lc_at k v ✓.
    Case false: result bvar i; need i < k; from lc_at (S k) (bvar i) : i < S k; and k ≠ i so i < k ✓ (Nat.lt_neq? we can use: i < S k and i ≠ k → i < k. Actually if i < S k then i <= k; if i ≠ k then i < k ✓ via Nat.lt_le? Use `Nat.lt_le_pred` or lia).
  - fvar ✓; app: IH; abs: open_rec k v (abs T t1) = abs T (open_rec (S k) v t1); need lc_at k (abs ...) ← lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) v t1) ← IH with k := S k, v, needing lc_at (S (S k)) t1 (from lc_at (S k) (abs T t1) → lc_at (S (S k)) t1) and lc_at (S k) v (from lc_at k v via mono) ✓.
  - true/false ✓.

  Lemma lc_open_fvar_inv : forall k body x, lc_at k (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) body) -> lc_at (S k) body.
  Induction on body generalizing k:
  - bvar i: open_rec k (fvar x) (bvar i) = if k =? i then fvar x else bvar i.
    Case true: lc_at k (fvar x) — always true; need lc_at (S k) (bvar i) with i = k → k < S k ✓.
    Case false: lc_at k (bvar i) → i < k → i < S k ✓.
  - fvar y: open gives fvar y; need lc_at (S k) (fvar y) ✓.
  - app: IH on both with the same k ✓.
  - abs: open_rec k (fvar x) (abs T t1) = abs T (open_rec (S k) (fvar x) t1); lc_at k of that gives lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) (fvar x) t1); IH with k := S k gives lc_at (S (S k)) t1; lc_abs gives lc_at (S k) (abs T t1) ✓.
  - true/false ✓.

  Lemma lc_subst : forall k t sigma, lc_at k t -> (forall x, lc_at k (sigma x)) -> lc_at k (substf sigma t).
  Induction on t generalizing k:
  - bvar i → need lc_at k (bvar i): from lc_at k (bvar i) ✓ i < k.
  - fvar x → lc_at k (sigma x) ✓.
  - app → IH both ✓.
  - abs T t1: need lc_at k (abs T (substf sigma t1)) ← lc_at (S k) (substf sigma t1) ← IH with k := S k; need (forall x, lc_at (S k) (sigma x)) from forall x, lc_at k (sigma x) via mono ✓.
  - true/false ✓.

  Lemma typing_lc : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> lc_at 0 t.
  Induction on has_type.
  - T_Var: lc_fvar ✓
  - T_Abs: need lc_at 0 (abs T1 t1) ← lc_at 1 t1. From IH for a fresh x: lc_at 0 (open t1 (fvar x)) — wait, the IH is forall x, ~ In x L -> lc_at 0 (open t1 (fvar x)) (since P Gamma t T := lc_at 0 t). Then apply lc_open_fvar_inv with k=0: lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 (fvar x) t1) → lc_at 1 t1 ✓.
    Pick x := S (max_atom L) with freshness ✓ (need only ~ In x L; also the freshness for lc_open_fvar_inv doesn't matter — it holds for any x). ✓
  - T_App: lc_app ✓
  - true/false ✓

  Lemma abs_no_step : forall T body t', ~ step (tm_abs T body) t'.
  Proof. intros T body t' H; inversion H. Qed.

  Same for true/false.

  Hmm, careful: `inversion H` where H : step (tm_abs T body) t'. The step constructors' conclusions are tm_app ... --> ..., which don't unify with tm_abs ... --> t'. Inversion should close the goal (no cases). ✓ Let me double check: `Inductive step : tm -> tm -> Prop` with constructors having conclusions like `tm_app (tm_abs T t) v --> open t v`. Inversion on `step (tm_abs T body) t'` — Coq will try to unify `tm_app _ _` with `tm_abs _ _` and fail, closing the goal. ✓

  ```
  Lemma subst_open_rec_fvar : forall sigma x w t k,

  Induction on t generalizing k.
  - bvar i: as analyzed, uses subst_update x x = w: `subst_update sigma x w x = if x =? x then w else sigma x = w` ✓ by Nat.eqb_refl.
  - fvar y: need ~ In x (fvars (fvar y)) → x ≠ y. Then (x =? y) = false by Nat.eqb_neq ✓.

  Now the "reducts" part: forall s, step (app t u) s -> R T2 s. Cases:

  - App1: app t' u with step t t'; R T t' from Hred; arrow clause with HRu ✓.
  - App2: IH_inner t2' Hstep2 (R_step T1 u t2' HRu Hstep2) ✓.

  Good.

  Now, R_backward's inner induction requires having SN u; we get it from R_sn HRu. Let me write the proof:

  ```
  Lemma R_backward : forall T t,
    lc_at 0 t ->
    strongly_normalizing t ->
    (forall t', step t t' -> R T t') ->
    (forall T0 body, t <> tm_abs T0 body) ->
    R T t.
  Proof.
    induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t Hlc Hsn Hred Hnotabs.
    - simpl. split; assumption.
    - simpl. split; [assumption|]. split; [assumption|].
      intros u HRu.
      assert (Hmain : forall u, strongly_normalizing u -> R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u)).
      { intros u HSN. induction HSN as [u Hstep IH].
        intros HRu.
        apply IH2 with (t := tm_app t u).
        + apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | apply R_lc with (T := T1); exact HRu].
        + apply SN_intro. intros s Hs. inversion Hs as [T0 body0 v Hlc0 Hval | t1 t1' t2 Hst Hlct2 | v1 t2 t2' Hval Hst].
          * (* AppAbs *) exfalso. apply (Hnotabs T0 body0). 
            (* from the equation tm_app t u = tm_app (tm_abs T0 body0) v *)
            injection H... 
          * (* App1 *) apply R_sn with (T := T2). 
            (* t1 = t, t1' = t1'? *)
            exact (proj2 (proj2 (Hred t1' Hst)) u HRu).  
            (* Hred t1' Hst : R (T1->T2) t1'; simpl it *)
          * (* App2 *) apply R_sn with (T := T2). apply IH. exact Hst. apply R_step with (t := u); assumption.
        + intros s Hs. inversion Hs as [...].
          * AppAbs: contradiction.
          * App1: apply (proj2 (proj2 (Hred t1' Hst)) u HRu).
          * App2: apply IH; [exact Hst | apply R_step ...].
        + discriminate. (* or intros T0 body0 H; discriminate H *)
      }
      apply Hmain; [apply R_sn with (T := T1); exact HRu | exact HRu].
  Qed.
  ```

  The inversion naming and equation handling will need work. Let me anticipate how `inversion Hs` behaves with the app term. Hs : step (tm_app t u) s. Inversion with ST_AppAbs: Coq will unify tm_app t u with tm_app (tm_abs T0 body0) v, giving t = tm_abs T0 body0 and u = v, and s = open body0 v. So after inversion, we have an equation (or the hypotheses substituted). In Coq, `inversion Hs` typically substitutes the equations in the goal and context automatically when possible. Since t is a variable and tm_abs T0 body0 a term, Coq might rewrite t := tm_abs T0 body0 in the context (if t occurs), leading to Hnotabs T0 body0 eq_refl : False? Let's see: Hnotabs : forall T0 body, t <> tm_abs T0 body. After inversion substitutes t, Hnotabs becomes forall T0 body, tm_abs T0 body <> tm_abs T0 body — then `exfalso; exact (Hnotabs T0 body0 eq_refl)`. Might need adjusting. We'll see in practice.

  Alternatively, to make life easier, I can write explicit inversion patterns with `inversion Hs as [...]` and then use `subst`. Let's iterate during compilation.

  Another approach to avoid inversion naming pain: define helper lemmas characterizing steps of an application:
  ```
  Lemma step_app_inv : forall t u s, step (tm_app t u) s ->
    (exists T0 body v, t = tm_abs T0 body /\ u = v /\ s = open body v) \/
    (exists t', step t t' /\ s = tm_app t' u) \/
    (exists u', value t /\ step u u' /\ s = tm_app t u').
  ```
  Hmm, that's more work; inversion directly is fine.

  Lemma abs_app_all : forall u, strongly_normalizing u ->
    forall T1 T2 body,
      lc_at 0 (tm_abs T1 body) ->
      (forall w, R T1 w -> R T2 (open body w)) ->

      + (* AppAbs *) apply R_sn with (T := T2). (* s = open t0 v *) apply Hbody. exact HRu.
        (* need t0 = body, v = u *)
      + (* App1 *) exfalso. eapply abs_no_step. exact Hst.
      + (* App2 *) apply R_sn with (T := T2). eapply IH; [exact Hst | exact Hlc | exact Hbody | apply R_step with (t := u); assumption].
    - (* reducts *) intros s Hs. inversion Hs as [...].
      + AppAbs: apply Hbody; exact HRu.
      + App1: exfalso; eapply abs_no_step; exact Hst.
      + App2: eapply IH; ...
    - (* not_abs *) intros T0 body0 H; discriminate H.
  Qed.
  ```
  Note: `ABS_app_all` requires `strongly_normalizing u` — supplied ✓.

  (** ** Auxiliary definitions and lemmas for the normalization proof. *)

  (* Free variables of a term *)
  Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
    match t with
    | tm_bvar _ => nil
    | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
    | tm_app t1 t2 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2
    | tm_abs _ t1 => fvars t1
    | tm_true => nil
    | tm_false => nil
    end.

  Fixpoint max_atom (l : list atom) : atom :=
    match l with
    | nil => 0
    | a :: l' => Nat.max a (max_atom l')
    end.

  Lemma max_atom_spec : forall l a, In a l -> a <= max_atom l.
  Proof.
    induction l as [| b l IH]; intros a Ha.
    - inversion Ha.
    - simpl. destruct Ha as [-> | Ha].
      + apply Nat.le_max_l.
      + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_atom l); [apply IH; exact Ha | apply Nat.le_max_r].
  Qed.

  Lemma fresh_atom : forall l, ~ In (S (max_atom l)) l.
  Proof.
    intros l Hin.
    pose proof (max_atom_spec l (S (max_atom l)) Hin) as Hle.
    lia.
  Qed.
  ```

  Fixpoint In (a:A) (l:list A) : Prop :=
    match l with
    | [] => False
    | b :: m => b = a \/ In a m

  Let me write the helper block:

```coq
(** ** Auxiliary definitions for the normalization proof. *)

Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvars t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Fixpoint max_atom (l : list atom) : atom :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | a :: l' => Nat.max a (max_atom l')
  end.

Lemma max_atom_spec : forall l a, In a l -> a <= max_atom l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| b l IH]; intros a Ha.
  - destruct Ha.
  - simpl in Ha. simpl. destruct Ha as [Hba | Ha].
    + subst a. apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_atom l).
      * apply IH; exact Ha.
      * apply Nat.le_max_r.
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall l, ~ In (S (max_atom l)) l.
Proof.
  intros l Hin. pose proof (max_atom_spec l (S (max_atom l)) Hin). lia.
Qed.

Definition subst_id : atom -> tm := fun x => tm_fvar x.

Definition subst_update (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (w : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then w else sigma y.

Fixpoint substf (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (substf sigma t1) (substf sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (substf sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Lemma substf_id : forall t, substf subst_id t = t.
Proof.
  induction t as [i|x|t1 IH1 t2 IH2|T t1 IH| |]; simpl.
  - reflexivity. - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IH1, IH2. reflexivity.
  - rewrite IH. reflexivity.
  - reflexivity. - reflexivity.
Qed.

Lemma lc_at_mono : forall t k k', k <= k' -> lc_at k t -> lc_at k' t.
Proof.
  induction t as [i|x|t1 IH1 t2 IH2|T t1 IH| |]; intros k k' Hle Hlc; simpl in *.
  - apply lc_bvar. inversion Hlc; lia.
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_app.
    + apply IH1 with (k := k); assumption.
    + apply IH2 with (k := k); assumption.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IH with (k := S k); [lia | inversion Hlc; assumption].
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.

Lemma open_rec_lc : forall t k u, lc_at k t -> open_rec k u t = t.
Proof.
  induction t as [i|x|t1 IH1 t2 IH2|T t1 IH| |]; intros k u Hlc; simpl.
  - assert (Hneq : (k =? i) = false) by (apply Nat.eqb_neq; inversion Hlc; lia).
    rewrite Hneq. reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IH1; [rewrite IH2 | inversion Hlc; assumption | inversion Hlc; assumption]; reflexivity.
  - rewrite IH; [reflexivity | inversion Hlc; assumption].
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.

Lemma lc_open_rec : forall t k v, lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k v -> lc_at k (open_rec k v t).
Proof.
  induction t as [i|x|t1 IH1 t2 IH2|T t1 IH| |]; intros k v Ht Hv; simpl.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k i) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst i. exact Hv.
    + apply lc_bvar. apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. inversion Ht; lia.
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_app.
    + apply IH1; [inversion Ht; assumption | exact Hv].
    + apply IH2; [inversion Ht; assumption | exact Hv].
  - apply lc_abs. apply IH.
    + inversion Ht; assumption.
    + apply lc_at_mono with (k := k); [lia | exact Hv].
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.

Lemma lc_open_fvar_inv : forall t k x, lc_at k (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t) -> lc_at (S k) t.
Proof.
  induction t as [i|x|t1 IH1 t2 IH2|T t1 IH| |]; intros k y H; simpl in H.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k i) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst i. apply lc_bvar. lia.
    + apply lc_bvar. apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. inversion H; lia.
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_app.
    + apply IH1 with (k := k); assumption.
    + apply IH2 with (k := k); assumption.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IH with (k := S k). assumption.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.

Lemma lc_subst : forall t k (sigma : atom -> tm),
  lc_at k t -> (forall x, lc_at k (sigma x)) -> lc_at k (substf sigma t).
Proof.
  induction t as [i|x|t1 IH1 t2 IH2|T t1 IH| |]; intros k sigma Hlc Hsig; simpl.
  - exact Hlc.
  - apply Hsig.
  - apply lc_app.
    + apply IH1; assumption.
    + apply IH2; assumption.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IH.
    + inversion Hlc; assumption.
    + intros y. apply lc_at_mono with (k := k); [lia | apply Hsig].
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.
```

Wait, in `lc_open_fvar_inv`'s abs case: after `simpl in H`, H : lc_at k (tm_abs T (open_rec (S k) (tm_fvar x) t1)). Then `apply lc_abs; apply IH with (k := S k); assumption`? The goal is lc_at (S k) (tm_abs T t1) — apply lc_abs gives lc_at (S (S k)) t1. IH t1 (S k) x : lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) (fvar x) t1) -> lc_at (S (S k)) t1. And the needed hypothesis is lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) (fvar x) t1) which comes from inversion of H ✓.

Now the R relation and its lemmas:

```coq
(** Reducibility / logical relation. *)

Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.

Lemma R_lc : forall T t, R T t -> lc_at 0 t.
Proof.
  intros T t HR. destruct T as [| T1 T2]; simpl in HR; tauto.
Qed.

Lemma R_sn : forall T t, R T t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros T t HR. destruct T as [| T1 T2]; simpl in HR; tauto.
Qed.
```
Hmm, `tauto` on `lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t` for the first conjunct: `tauto` should solve `(A /\ B) -> A` ✓. And `(A /\ B /\ C) -> A` and -> B ✓. But does `simpl in HR` reduce R? Let's test. If not, use `cbn [R] in HR` or `change`.

Actually, `destruct T as [| T1 T2]` then `simpl in HR`: `R (Ty_Bool) t` — a Fixpoint applied to a constructor — should reduce by iota ✓.

But there's a subtlety: R is defined by structural recursion on T; when T = Ty_Arrow T1 T2 with variables T1 T2, R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t reduces to the body with R T1 and R T2 still unapplied ✓. Good.

```coq
Lemma R_step : forall T t t', R T t -> step t t' -> R T t'.
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t t' HR Hst; simpl in HR.
  - destruct HR as [Hlc Hsn]. split.
    + apply step_lc with (t := t); assumption.
    + inversion Hsn as [t0 Hsucc]. apply Hsucc. exact Hst.
  - destruct HR as [Hlc [Hsn Hcl]]. split.
    + apply step_lc with (t := t); assumption.
    + split.
      * inversion Hsn as [t0 Hsucc]. apply Hsucc. exact Hst.
      * intros u HRu. apply IH2 with (t := tm_app t u).
        { apply Hcl. exact HRu. }
        { apply ST_App1. exact Hst. apply R_lc with (T := T1). exact HRu. }
Qed.
```

Hmm, `apply IH2 with (t := tm_app t u)` may cause confusion: IH2's variables are also named t and t'. Let me write `apply IH2 with (t := tm_app t u) (t' := tm_app t' u)`? That could conflict since IH2's own names. Better: `eapply IH2.` then provide subgoals. Let me write:

        -- apply Hcl. exact HRu.
        -- apply ST_App1; [exact Hst | apply R_lc with (T := T1); exact HRu].
```
`eapply IH2` will unify R T2 (tm_app t' u) with the conclusion R T2 ?y (where ?x --> ?y), instantiating ?y := tm_app t' u and ?x must satisfy step ?x (tm_app t' u) = ... we then give the step as ST_App1 with the right form, which will force ?x = tm_app t u ✓. Coq's unification should figure ?x from the step goal only after we provide ST_App1. OK, unification handles it.

Now R_backward:

```coq
Lemma R_backward : forall T t,
  lc_at 0 t ->
  strongly_normalizing t ->
  (forall t', step t t' -> R T t') ->
  (forall T0 body, t <> tm_abs T0 body) ->
  R T t.
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t Hlc Hsn Hred Hnotabs.
  - simpl. split; assumption.
  - simpl. split; [assumption | split; [assumption |]].
    intros u HRu.
    assert (Hmain : forall u, strongly_normalizing u -> R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u)).
    { intros u HSN. induction HSN as [u Hstep IH].
      intros HRu.
      assert (Happs : forall s, step (tm_app t u) s -> R T2 s).
      { intros s Hs. inversion Hs as [T0 body0 v Hlc0 Hval | t1 t1' t2 Hst Hlct2 | v1 t2 t2' Hval Hst].
        - (* ST_AppAbs *) exfalso. apply (Hnotabs T0 body0). 
          (* need t = tm_abs T0 body0 from the equation *)
          admit.
        - (* ST_App1 *) 
          (* t1 = t, t1' = ... *)
          eapply Hred_arrow... 
        - (* ST_App2 *)
          eapply IH; ...

      apply IH2 with (t := tm_app t u).
      + apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | apply R_lc with (T := T1); exact HRu].
      + apply SN_intro. intros s Hs. apply R_sn with (T := T2). apply Happs. exact Hs.
      + exact Happs.
      + intros T0 body0 Heq. discriminate Heq.
    }
    apply Hmain.
    + apply R_sn with (T := T1); exact HRu.
    + exact HRu.
Qed.
```

```coq
Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  <{ Gamma |-- t \in T }> ->
  forall sigma,
    (forall y, lc_at 0 (sigma y)) ->
    (forall x S, Gamma x = Some S -> R S (sigma x)) ->
    R T (substf sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T H. induction H as [Gamma x T Hx | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 IH | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 | Gamma | Gamma]; intros sigma HsigLc Hsig.

Lemma fresh_app : forall l1 l2, ~ In (S (max_atom (l1 ++ l2))) l1 /\ ~ In (S (max_atom (l1 ++ l2))) l2.

Something like that. Let me define:
```
Lemma not_in_app_l : forall l1 l2, ~ In (S (max_atom (l1 ++ l2))) l1.
Proof.
  intros l1 l2 Hin. 
  assert (Hin' : In (S (max_atom (l1 ++ l2))) (l1 ++ l2)) by (apply in_or_app; left; exact Hin).
  pose proof (max_atom_spec (l1 ++ l2) _ Hin'). lia.
Qed.
Lemma not_in_app_r : forall l1 l2, ~ In (S (max_atom (l1 ++ l2))) l2.
Proof.
  intros l1 l2 Hin. 
  assert (Hin' : In (S (max_atom (l1 ++ l2))) (l1 ++ l2)) by (apply in_or_app; right; exact Hin).
  pose proof (max_atom_spec (l1 ++ l2) _ Hin'). lia.
Qed.
```
Note: `max_atom_spec (l1 ++ l2) (S (max_atom (l1 ++ l2)))` requires the `In` proof as an argument — the lemma is `forall l a, In a l -> a <= max_atom l`, so pose proof (max_atom_spec (l1++l2) (S (...)) Hin') gives `S (...) <= max_atom (...)`; lia ✓.

(Helper block begins)
```coq
(** ** Auxiliary definitions and lemmas for the normalization proof. *)

(** Free variables of a term, and a fresh-atom generator. *)

Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvars t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Fixpoint max_atom (l : list atom) : atom :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | a :: l' => Nat.max a (max_atom l')
  end.

Lemma max_atom_spec : forall l a, In a l -> a <= max_atom l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| b l IH]; intros a Ha.
  - destruct Ha.
  - simpl in Ha. simpl. destruct Ha as [Hba | Ha].
    + subst a. apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_atom l).
      * apply IH; exact Ha.
      * apply Nat.le_max_r.
Qed.

Definition fresh (l : list atom) : atom := S (max_atom l).

Lemma fresh_notin : forall l, ~ In (fresh l) l.
Proof.
  intros l Hin. unfold fresh in Hin.
  pose proof (max_atom_spec l (S (max_atom l)) Hin). lia.
Qed.

Lemma fresh_notin_app_l : forall l1 l2, ~ In (fresh (l1 ++ l2)) l1.
Proof.
  intros l1 l2 Hin.
  assert (Hin' : In (fresh (l1 ++ l2)) (l1 ++ l2)).
  { apply in_or_app. left. exact Hin. }
  pose proof (fresh_notin (l1 ++ l2) Hin'). exact H.
Qed.

Lemma fresh_notin_app_r : forall l1 l2, ~ In (fresh (l1 ++ l2)) l2.
Proof.
  intros l1 l2 Hin.
  assert (Hin' : In (fresh (l1 ++ l2)) (l1 ++ l2)).
  { apply in_or_app. right. exact Hin. }
  pose proof (fresh_notin (l1 ++ l2) Hin'). exact H.
Qed.
```
Hmm, `pose proof (fresh_notin (l1 ++ l2) Hin')` gives H : ~ In (fresh (l1++l2)) (l1++l2), and the goal is ~ In (fresh (l1++l2)) l1 — but we already have Hin : In ... l1. So `exact (H Hin')` ✓. Let me write `exact (fresh_notin (l1 ++ l2) Hin')` ✓.

Continue:
```coq
(** Substitution of free variables. *)

Definition subst_id : atom -> tm := fun x => tm_fvar x.

Definition subst_update (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (w : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then w else sigma y.

Fixpoint substf (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (substf sigma t1) (substf sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (substf sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Lemma substf_id : forall t, substf subst_id t = t.
Proof.
  induction t as [i|x|t1 IH1 t2 IH2|T t1 IH| |]; simpl.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IH1, IH2. reflexivity.
  - rewrite IH. reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.

(** Local-closure lemmas. *)

Lemma lc_at_mono : forall t k k', k <= k' -> lc_at k t -> lc_at k' t.
Proof.

    + lia.
    + inversion Hlc; assumption.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.

Lemma open_rec_lc : forall t k u, lc_at k t -> open_rec k u t = t.
Proof.
  induction t as [i|x|t1 IH1 t2 IH2|T t1 IH| |]; intros k u Hlc; simpl.

    { apply Nat.eqb_neq. inversion Hlc. lia. }

  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IH1. rewrite IH2. reflexivity.
    + inversion Hlc; assumption.
    + inversion Hlc; assumption.
  - rewrite IH. reflexivity. inversion Hlc; assumption.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.
```

(** ** Logical relation (reducibility) for strong normalization. *)

Fixpoint Red (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall u, Red T1 u -> Red T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.

Lemma Red_lc : forall T t, Red T t -> lc_at 0 t.

  intros T t HR. destruct T as [| T1 T2]; simpl in HR; tauto.
Qed.

Lemma Red_sn : forall T t, Red T t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros T t HR. destruct T as [| T1 T2]; simpl in HR; tauto.
Qed.
```

First Red and basic lemmas:

```coq
(** ** The logical relation. *)

Fixpoint Red (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      lc_at 0 t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall u, Red T1 u -> Red T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.

Lemma Red_lc : forall T t, Red T t -> lc_at 0 t.
Proof.
  intros T t HR. destruct T as [| T1 T2]; simpl in HR.
  - exact (proj1 HR).
  - exact (proj1 HR).
Qed.

Lemma Red_sn : forall T t, Red T t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros T t HR. destruct T as [| T1 T2]; simpl in HR.
  - exact (proj2 HR).
  - exact (proj1 (proj2 HR)).
Qed.

Lemma Red_step : forall T t t', Red T t -> step t t' -> Red T t'.
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t t' HR Hst; simpl in HR.
  - destruct HR as [Hlc Hsn]. split.
    + apply step_lc with (t := t); assumption.
    + inversion Hsn as [t0 Hsucc]. apply Hsucc. exact Hst.
  - destruct HR as [Hlc [Hsn Hcl]]. split.
    + apply step_lc with (t := t); assumption.
    + split.
      * inversion Hsn as [t0 Hsucc]. apply Hsucc. exact Hst.
      * intros u HRu.
        assert (Hstep' : step (tm_app t u) (tm_app t' u)).
        { apply ST_App1.
          - exact Hst.
          - apply Red_lc with (T := T1). exact HRu. }
        specialize (IH2 (tm_app t u) (tm_app t' u)).
        apply IH2; [apply Hcl; exact HRu | exact Hstep'].
Qed.
```

Wait, in the first branch (Ty_Bool), `destruct HR as [Hlc Hsn]` then the goal is `lc_at 0 t' /\ SN t'`. But careful: `simpl in HR` — does it reduce `Red Ty_Bool t` to the conjunction? Red is a Fixpoint; with the type argument being the constructor Ty_Bool (after destruct T), yes.

Hmm, but after `destruct T as [| T1 T2]`, does the goal's Red still refer to T? The goal is `Red T t'`; after destruct, it becomes `Red Ty_Bool t'` / `Red (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t'` ✓, and `simpl in HR` reduces HR's type. But the goal also needs simplification to match the split: `split` on goal `Red Ty_Bool t'` — is it a conjunction syntactically? Red Ty_Bool t' is a Fixpoint application that reduces (delta+iota) to the conjunction; `split` might work by conversion, but safer to `simpl` the goal too. Let me do `simpl in *` or `simpl in HR ⊢`. In Coq: `simpl in HR |- *`? Syntax: `simpl in HR |- *` simplifies HR and the goal. Let me just `simpl in *` which simplifies hypotheses and goal ✓.

Careful: `simpl in *` might over-simplify other hypotheses, but should be OK.

Now Red_backward:

```coq
Lemma Red_backward : forall T t,
  lc_at 0 t ->
  strongly_normalizing t ->
  (forall t', step t t' -> Red T t') ->
  (forall T0 body, t <> tm_abs T0 body) ->
  Red T t.
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t Hlc Hsn Hred Hnotabs.
  - simpl. split; assumption.
  - simpl. split; [assumption | split; [assumption |]].
    intros u HRu.
    assert (Hmain : forall u, strongly_normalizing u -> Red T1 u -> Red T2 (tm_app t u)).
    { intros u HSN. induction HSN as [u Hstep IH].
      intros HRu.
      assert (Happs : forall s, step (tm_app t u) s -> Red T2 s).
      { intros s Hs.
        inversion Hs as [T0 body0 v Hlc0 Hval | t1 t1' t2 Hst Hlct2 | v1 t2 t2' Hval Hst].
        - (* ST_AppAbs *) subst. exfalso. apply (Hnotabs T0 body0). reflexivity.
        - (* ST_App1 *) subst.
          assert (HRt' : Red (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t1') by (apply Hred; exact Hst).
          simpl in HRt'. apply (proj2 (proj2 HRt')). exact HRu.
        - (* ST_App2 *) subst.
          apply IH.
          + exact Hst.
          + apply Red_step with (t := u); assumption.
      }
      apply IH2 with (t := tm_app t u).
      + apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | apply Red_lc with (T := T1); exact HRu].
      + apply SN_intro. intros s Hs. apply Red_sn with (T := T2). apply Happs. exact Hs.
      + exact Happs.
      + intros T0 body0 Heq. discriminate Heq.
    }
    apply Hmain.
    + apply Red_sn with (T := T1); exact HRu.
    + exact HRu.
Qed.
```

Now abs_app_red:

```coq
Lemma abs_app_red : forall u, strongly_normalizing u ->
  forall T1 T2 body,
    lc_at 0 (tm_abs T1 body) ->
    (forall w, Red T1 w -> Red T2 (open body w)) ->
    Red T1 u ->
    Red T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) u).
Proof.
  intros u HSN. induction HSN as [u Hstep IH].
  intros T1 T2 body Hlc Hbody HRu.
  apply Red_backward with (T := T2).
  - apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | apply Red_lc with (T := T1); exact HRu].
  - apply SN_intro. intros s Hs.
    inversion Hs as [T0 body0 v Hlc0 Hval | t1 t1' t2 Hst Hlct2 | v1 t2 t2' Hval Hst].
    + subst. apply Red_sn with (T := T2). apply Hbody. exact HRu.
    + subst. exfalso. apply (abs_no_step T1 body t1'). exact Hst.
    + subst. apply Red_sn with (T := T2). apply IH.
      * exact Hst.
      * exact Hlc.
      * exact Hbody.
      * apply Red_step with (t := u); assumption.
  - intros s Hs.
    inversion Hs as [T0 body0 v Hlc0 Hval | t1 t1' t2 Hst Hlct2 | v1 t2 t2' Hval Hst].
    + subst. apply Hbody. exact HRu.
    + subst. exfalso. apply (abs_no_step T1 body t1'). exact Hst.
    + subst. apply IH.
      * exact Hst.
      * exact Hlc.
      * exact Hbody.
      * apply Red_step with (t := u); assumption.
  - intros T0 body0 Heq. discriminate Heq.
Qed.
```

Now the fundamental theorem. Let me write it.

```coq
Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  <{ Gamma |-- t \in T }> ->
  forall sigma,
    (forall y, lc_at 0 (sigma y)) ->
    (forall x S, Gamma x = Some S -> Red S (sigma x)) ->
    Red T (substf sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T H. induction H as
    [Gamma x T Hx | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hprem IH | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 | Gamma | Gamma];
    intros sigma HsigLc Hsig.
  - simpl. apply Hsig. exact Hx.
  - simpl.
    assert (Hlcabs : lc_at 0 (tm_abs T1 t1)).
    { eapply typing_lc. eapply T_Abs. eassumption. }
    split.
    + (* lc *) apply lc_abs. apply (lc_subst t1 1 sigma).
      * inversion Hlcabs. assumption.
      * intros y. apply lc_at_mono with (k := 0); [lia | apply HsigLc].
    + split.
      * (* SN *) apply SN_intro. intros t' Hst. exact (abs_no_step T1 (substf sigma t1) t' Hst).
      * (* applications *) intros u HRu.
        apply (abs_app_red u).
        -- apply Red_sn with (T := T1). exact HRu.
        -- (* lc abs *) apply lc_abs. apply (lc_subst t1 1 sigma).
           ++ inversion Hlcabs. assumption.
           ++ intros y. apply lc_at_mono with (k := 0); [lia | apply HsigLc].
        -- (* semantic body *) intros w HRw.
           assert (Hlcw : lc_at 0 w) by (apply Red_lc with (T := T1); exact HRw).
           assert (HsigLc' : forall y, lc_at 0 (subst_update sigma (fresh (L ++ fvars t1)) w y)).
           { intros y. unfold subst_update. destruct (Nat.eqb (fresh (L ++ fvars t1)) y).
             - exact Hlcw.
             - apply HsigLc. }
           assert (Hctx' : forall y S,
             update Gamma (fresh (L ++ fvars t1)) T1 y = Some S ->
             Red S (subst_update sigma (fresh (L ++ fvars t1)) w y)).
           { intros y S Hy. unfold update in Hy.
             destruct (Nat.eqb (fresh (L ++ fvars t1)) y) eqn:E.
             - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y. inversion Hy. subst S.
               unfold subst_update. simpl. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. exact HRw.
             - apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. unfold subst_update. simpl. rewrite E.
               apply Hsig. exact Hy. }
           pose proof (IH (fresh (L ++ fvars t1)) (fresh_notin_app_l L (fvars t1))
                        (subst_update sigma (fresh (L ++ fvars t1)) w) HsigLc' Hctx') as Hsem.
           assert (Hcomm : substf (subst_update sigma (fresh (L ++ fvars t1)) w)
                             (open t1 (tm_fvar (fresh (L ++ fvars t1)))) =
                           open (substf sigma t1) w).
           { apply subst_open_rec_fvar; [exact HsigLc | exact Hlcw | exact (fresh_notin_app_r L (fvars t1))]. }
           rewrite Hcomm in Hsem. exact Hsem.
        -- exact HRu.
  - (* T_App *) simpl. pose proof (IH1 sigma HsigLc Hsig) as Hf. simpl in Hf.
     destruct Hf as [Hlcf [Hsnf Hclf]]. apply (Hclf (substf sigma t2)). apply IH2; assumption.
  - (* T_True *) simpl. split.
    + apply lc_true.
    + apply SN_intro. intros t' Hst. exact (true_no_step t' Hst).
  - (* T_False *) simpl. split.
    + apply lc_false.
    + apply SN_intro. intros t' Hst. exact (false_no_step t' Hst).
Qed.
```

Wait: in the T_Abs case, `Hlcabs` is obtained by `eapply typing_lc. eapply T_Abs. eassumption.` Hmm, `typing_lc` is `forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> lc_at 0 t`; `eapply typing_lc` creates goal `has_type ?Gamma ?t ?T`; `eapply T_Abs` turns it into `forall x, ~ In x ?L -> has_type (update ?Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2` — using our known t1 T1 T2? Now the final theorem:

```coq
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T H.
  assert (HsigLc : forall y, lc_at 0 (subst_id y)).
  { intros y. apply lc_fvar. }
  assert (Hctx : forall x S, empty x = Some S -> Red S (subst_id x)).
  { intros x S Hx. unfold empty in Hx. discriminate Hx. }
  pose proof (fundamental empty t T H subst_id HsigLc Hctx) as HR.
  rewrite substf_id in HR.
  exact (Red_sn T t HR).
Qed.
```

Hmm, one thing: `unfold empty in Hx` — empty is a Definition; Hx : empty x = Some S; after unfold: (fun _ => None) x = Some S which beta-reduces to None = Some S. Does `unfold empty in Hx` produce `(fun _ : atom => None) x = Some S`? Then `discriminate Hx` should still work (β-conversion) ✓. Alternatively `simpl in Hx`.

Now, `HsigLc : forall y, lc_at 0 (subst_id y)` — subst_id y = tm_fvar y definitionally; lc_fvar 0 y : lc_at 0 (tm_fvar y) ✓.

One concern: The `fundamental` theorem's sigma argument: `fundamental empty t T H subst_id HsigLc Hctx` — but the theorem statement has `forall Gamma t T, <{...}> -> forall sigma, ...`. Applying to empty t T H subst_id ✓.
